Yoon Jong muttered while sprawled on the ground.

“I think my lifespan has been shortened by at least a year...”

“For me, three years.”

“I lost five years...”

Everyone shook their heads with drained faces. Yet, they couldn’t fully relax, constantly fearing that those Four Sects Alliance bastards might change their minds and return at any moment.

‘Jang Ilso.’

Baek Cheon gazed beyond the desolate land toward the horizon. If you think about it, this war began with the high priest Dan Ja-Kang and ended with Jang Ilso. The fierce martial prowess shown by Dan Ja-Kang was ultimately overshadowed by Jang Ilso.

‘And... that high priest.’

Baek Cheon bit his lip tightly without realizing it. Thinking of the one who pierced Dan Ja-Kang’s heart made his body tremble involuntarily.

‘Are there really so many monsters in this world?’

He seemed to understand why the murim world was called the Dragon’s Lair and Tiger’s Den. There were three monsters who each turned the murim world upside down. To see all three at once was hard to tell if it was a blessing or a curse.

‘No... it’s not three.’

Baek Cheon turned his head.

‘It’s four.’

He saw Chung Myung standing expressionlessly among them.

‘Come to think of it, this guy is truly amazing.’

At least Jang Ilso led Black Ghost Fortress and the Red Dogs, and the high priests led the Demonic Cult members. But Chung Myung only brought about a dozen people and shook the battlefield where monsters rampaged to devour each other.

‘I wonder how I would have felt if I were the enemy facing this guy?’

Perhaps those who faced Chung Myung left carrying something even greater than the fear they felt facing the high priest or the intimidation they felt facing Jang Ilso.

In times like this, he was reminded anew. Though so familiar that he often forgot, just how incredible this guy was.

“What?”

Feeling Baek Cheon’s steady gaze on him, Chung Myung asked gruffly. Baek Cheon hesitated briefly before speaking.

“Is your body alright?”

There were many things to ask, but the most important question was this. Though a bit late, it had to be asked.

Chung Myung chuckled upon hearing the question.

“Don’t worry about it...”

He spoke calmly but suddenly closed his mouth and tilted his head slightly.

“Why? Is someone coming?”

“No, it’s not that.”

“Then?”

“...No. I just felt something was a bit off.”

Just as Baek Cheon was about to ask what he meant, red blood gushed from Chung Myung’s nose and mouth. Baek Cheon’s eyes widened instantly.

“You... you, you... ah, no, why are you like this?”

“Huh?”

Reacting to Baek Cheon, Chung Myung reached out and wiped his face. Seeing the blood soaking his palm, his expression changed bewilderedly.

“...Huh?”

“You, you were cultivating your qi! Damn it! Why are you like this after cultivation is done?”

“...No. I was too busy regaining my internal qi... I barely did any healing through the cultivation...”

“What?”

Seeing the blood drain from Chung Myung’s face, the Mount Hua disciples were shocked and jumped up.

“No, it’s okay. This much is nothing... huh? Why am I dizzy...”

“So-Soso! Soso! That guy is dying!”

Jo Gul screamed as he saw Chung Myung staggering backward, dizzy.

“Hey, you crazy bastard!”

Before his scream even ended, Tang Soso had already rolled her eyes back and lunged at Chung Myung.

Chung Myung lay back staring at the sky, emptiness in his eyes. His lips moved slightly.

“Um...”

Thud!

“Ugh...”

A large acupuncture needle pierced the center of his upper lip as Chung Myung rolled his eyes and trembled.

“W-why are you sticking a needle in my lip?! This has nothing to do with healing!”

“It’s a mouth treatment technique I developed. The mouth is the biggest problem for my sasuk.”

“No!”

Thud!

Before Chung Myung could say anything, another large needle pierced the center of his forehead.

“...Keep talking. Keep going.”

Tang Soso glared with the needle in hand, exuding a chilling aura like a hellish cold wind.

Chung Myung quietly closed his mouth. Surprisingly, even he had some sense, especially at times like this.

“No, this crazy person, I gave you so much time to cultivate qi, and what? You didn’t heal? Are you crazy wanting to die, you bastard?”

“Soso... but he is your sasuk...”

“What?”

“...There’s one spot left on the crown of his head for a needle.”

“Yes.”

Thud!

The moment the needle pierced his crown, Chung Myung twitched like a fish hooked on a fishing line. His tear-filled eyes glared at Baek Cheon with resentment. Baek Cheon avoided the gaze with a guilty face.

“I’m sorry.”

But isn’t it wiser not to avoid the blowing wind?

“By the way, sasuk.”

“Yes?”

Jo Gul asked incredulously.

“Usually... can cultivation be divided into recovering internal qi and healing the body separately?”

“Usually, no?”

For a moment, a complex emotion appeared on Jo Gul’s face. He thought while looking at Chung Myung: using such incredible skill like that is a skill in itself.

Un Geom stared at Chung Myung, who was trembling with needles stuck all over his body like a porcupine, and asked Tang Soso.

“How is he?”

“He’s a mess.”

Tang Soso said bluntly and then sighed deeply.

“He’s hardly human, so he’ll recover, but if he had fought more, he would have really died. No, does he even have any sense...”

“If not for that, it wouldn’t have been me dying, but everyone here would have died...”

Unable to watch the needles flying like rays of light toward Chung Myung’s mouth, Un Geom tightly closed his eyes.

Thud.

“Ugh...”

If only he kept his mouth shut, fewer needles would be stuck, but is that so hard...?

A deep sigh escaped from Un Geom’s mouth.

“It’s not something I can’t understand.”

Tang Soso was angry and frustrated with Chung Myung, but she probably knew too. If Chung Myung had ended his cultivation a bit later, who knew how the situation would have changed for them all.

Recovering internal qi and healing the body simultaneously in such a short time was impossible. Chung Myung had no other choice in that situation.

Tang Soso’s anger was probably due to her helplessness and guilt for pushing Chung Myung so hard, just as Un Geom was feeling now.

“No. Even when I saved her, she complains! Do you think there was another way if I hadn’t done this? These youngsters these days... ugh!”

Yu Yiseol, crouching by Chung Myung’s head, pressed one of the large needles deeper into his forehead with her fingertip.

“Sago. I understand your feelings, but if that goes in too deep, sahyung will really die.”

“That’s what I wanted.”

Tang Soso glanced at Yu Yiseol’s expressionless face and quietly lowered her eyes.

“...The issue at hand is sorted out.”

“What do we have to do if the issue starts up again?”

“...Still, he’ll survive.”

“Hmm.”

Yu Yiseol looked at Chung Myung with displeasure. Even the great Chung Myung couldn’t avoid her gaze at that moment.

“That crazy bastard, really.”

Baek Cheon, watching the scene, muttered and shook his head.

“Soso.”

“Yes, sasuk.”

“So, when will the treatment end?”

“Complete recovery here is impossible. It’s just enough to breathe life into him. That part is already done.”

“Hmm? Then why do you keep the needles in?”

“Shall I remove them?”

Baek Cheon hesitated briefly, then nodded heavily.

“Let’s keep them in a little longer.”

“Yes.”

Listening to the conversation of the Mount Hua disciples in the cold wind, Im So-Byeong suddenly shivered from the chill.

“...His mental stability must be painfully good.”

Looking at his clothes pierced with hundreds of holes, Chung Myung grimaced. Then Tang Soso grabbed the needles she had taken back once more.

“Why? Feeling cold? Should I plug those holes again?”

“...W-what did I say? I didn’t say anything.”

Chung Myung slowly backed away from Tang Soso. Personally, Tang Soso was scarier than the high priest at times like this.

“Anyway, really.”

Tang Soso gritted her teeth fiercely. The sound was terrifying. Im So-Byeong gave a bitter smile alone.

‘Should I say they owe a debt?’

Even if he hadn’t pushed this far, Jang Ilso would have let Chung Myung live. Im So-Byeong thought so. Chung Myung was someone Jang Ilso absolutely could not afford to lose at this point.

But Im So-Byeong knew that Jang Ilso only needed Chung Myung. The other Mount Hua disciples’ deaths didn’t matter. In fact, it might have been better if they died.

‘In my case, there’s no question.’

If he were Jang Ilso, he would have definitely killed Im So-Byeong here, by any means.

Knowing all this, Chung Myung tried to regain power that could threaten Jang Ilso, taking the risk of cultivating internal qi in the middle of this dangerous battlefield.

‘As a result...’

In the end, they succeeded in having not a single casualty in this brutal battlefield. Absurdly so.

Im So-Byeong had thought that Mount Hua having no casualties until now was just luck. Considering the battlefields they had faced, it was stranger if there were no casualties.

But after this battle, he knew for sure.

‘It wasn’t luck.’

Luck means something given unintentionally. The result of a leader being so thorough and meticulous is not called luck.

Im So-Byeong, leading a group himself, also knew how meaningless and difficult it was to exclude sacrifices in the murim world.

But the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint was actually accomplishing things that seemed impossible. Sometimes preparing, sometimes charging, sometimes risking their lives on adventures.

Im So-Byeong couldn’t even imagine how much Chung Myung had worried leading the Mount Hua sect this far.

“Truly an amazing person.”

Im So-Byeong looked at Chung Myung with renewed eyes. Chung Myung’s back standing tall on the desolate land felt immensely grand at this moment.

Chapter 1081: Since It Is The Duty Of The One Who Is A Taoist. (1)

“Phew. That was really dangerous this time. If we had slipped up, we would all have been dead.”

Baek Cheon blinked as he looked at Chung Myung, who was grinning slyly while speaking. Feeling Baek Cheon’s gaze, Chung Myung asked, “What?”

“...Nothing.”

After a brief silence, Baek Cheon shook his head, suppressing the urge to say, ‘I’m just curious what’s going on in your head that you can still laugh in a situation like this.’

“But... did the Four Sects Alliance really just leave?”

“Do you think they pretended to leave?”

“No. That’s not what I mean...”

Baek Cheon slightly turned his head to look toward the horizon—the direction where Jang Ilso and his group had disappeared. A subtle unease still lingered in his eyes.

“It seems too easy for that guy to have withdrawn.”

“That is an unavoidable choice even for Jang Ilso.”

Hearing the voice beside him, Baek Cheon turned his head. Im So-Byeong was holding a fully opened fan and wearing a meaningful smile.

“Because the fire has fallen on his feet.”

“...Fire?”

“Yes. More precisely, it’s the fire he himself started.”

Seeing Baek Cheon’s confused look, Im So-Byeong kindly explained.

“Jang Ilso was already gradually devouring the Pirates’ troops, whose Black Dragon King had fallen from power. Now, unexpectedly, the high priest Dan Ja-Kang has also fallen, so it’s no exaggeration to say his workload has doubled.”

“Hmm. Are you saying a rebellion might break out within the Four Sects Alliance?”

“Ah, that’s unlikely. Both the Pirates and the Black Ghost Fortress have lost their leaders. A rebellion requires a clear focal point. But...”

Im So-Byeong suddenly snapped his fluttering fan shut.

“Another scenario can happen.”

“Another scenario?”

“Yes. Defection.”

“Defection?”

Other Mount Hua disciples who were listening closely also looked at Im So-Byeong with puzzled expressions. He smiled slightly.

“It may be hard for those from the Justice faction to understand, but the Evil Sect members aren’t very loyal to their places. Many don’t even like being affiliated with their sect, and among them, quite a few will leave the sect whenever they get the chance.”

“Ah.”

“What holds them in place is the presence of their leader and the punishment for defectors that is enforced. So those afraid of falling out of favor with the leader try to leave the sect when it becomes chaotic.”

Baek Cheon nodded as if he understood.

“Indeed, it’s unimaginable for the Justice faction.”

“Conversely, from our perspective, people like Baek Cheon of Mount Hua are harder to understand. Mount Hua was already a ruined sect long ago, and staying there brought no benefits to anyone.”

“Because I wasn’t trying to gain anything.”

Im So-Byeong smiled bitterly at Baek Cheon’s calm answer.

This is probably a gap that can’t be bridged even by talking all day. It’s not a matter of right or wrong, but of values a person holds.

Im So-Byeong scratched his head with his fan.

“The conversation drifted a bit, but anyway, the Black Ghost Fortress also relied heavily on the Desolate Gold Elder’s control. Once news of his death spreads, chaos will naturally ensue. Many will try to take advantage or defect from the sect.”

“Indeed...”

“So Jang Ilso can’t help but be busy. What he wants is to absorb the Pirates and Black Ghost Fortress’s forces completely, leaving not a speck behind. He probably won’t have the mind to look elsewhere for a while. After all, the area he must directly manage has multiplied several times.”

Only then did everyone nod.

“On the other hand, it might be a fortunate story.”

“Yes?”

Im So-Byeong asked back as if wondering why that conclusion was drawn.

“Isn’t it that Jang Ilso will also find it difficult to move for a while? Even for him, completely controlling all the forces of the Black Ghost Fortress and Pirates won’t be easy, as you said.”

If domination means putting them underfoot, it’s not hard for Jang Ilso. But preventing defectors and fully managing their forces is more complicated than expected.

Even the great Jang Ilso will likely be tied down for a while.

“Indeed. That’s what you meant. It’s not wrong.”

Im So-Byeong nodded vigorously. Then Baek Cheon added to Yoon Jong’s words.

“Moreover... the Desolate Gold Elder died, and the Black Dragon King lost an arm, right? That’s like losing two absolute warriors who were already scarce on the Four Sects Alliance’s side, so their losses are significant.”

“That’s also true.”

Im So-Byeong smiled.

“Certainly... even if the Ten Thousand People Clan mobilizes all their power and uses all of Mount Hua’s strength, chaos is inevitable for now.”

“As expected...”

“But Yoon Jong, do you truly understand what that means?”

“Yes?”

Yoon Jong’s face looked a bit puzzled. Im So-Byeong spoke with a meaningful expression.

“Certainly... the Four Sects Alliance will suffer internal strife for a while. Considering Jang Ilso’s nature, a lot of blood might be spilled in the process. The Four Sects Alliance’s overall strength might even decrease compared to now.”

Everyone swallowed dryly at those words.

Such changes always come with bloodshed. Those who want to maintain the existing order always resist change.

But... Jang Ilso is not someone who will coax or appease them. He will crush them thoroughly so they never resist him again, just as he showed the Pirates an example at the Yangtze River.

“When all that ends, the Four Sects Alliance will be completely different from before. The loosely allied Four Sects Alliance will disappear from the world. In its place will appear a complete Evil Sect, moving in perfect unison at Jang Ilso’s command as a single sect.”

It will truly be the Evil Sect united under the Ten Thousand People Clan. Of course, they won’t use the name Ten Thousand People Clan again due to symbolism.

Somewhere, the smell of blood seems to linger.

“Since even the Lower District sect’s Lord has surrendered to Jang Ilso, no one can stop him anymore. At most half a year. Within that time, Jang Ilso will unite all the Four Sects Alliances under him. After that...”

Everyone knew what came next.

“Jang Ilso will surely eye his next prey. He is someone who never knows satisfaction.”

The phrase ‘at most half a year’ pierced everyone’s hearts.

Everyone here watched carefully to see what kind of man Jang Ilso was.

Dan Ja-Kang and Heaven Killer, the two high priests, had shown terrifying power. But the last thing etched in the minds of those present was, without doubt, Jang Ilso.

“Could it be cooperation...”

“No.”

Before Yoon Jong could finish his hesitant words, Chung Myung’s firm voice cut him off.

“That won’t happen.”

Everyone turned to Chung Myung.

“Jang Ilso is Jang Ilso. In the end, he’s just an Evil Sect bastard. Cooperation with such a guy was impossible from the start.”

“...But this time it was possible.”

“Because interests aligned. But they won’t cooperate with us to stop the Demonic Cult. Especially not when that guy leads the Evil Sect.”

Yoon Jong sighed.

He didn’t fully understand why Chung Myung spoke so decisively. But if he was that certain, then it was probably true. It always had been.

“Half a year...”

Chung Myung murmured quietly. His gaze darkened. In truth, even half a year was a generous estimate. He knew how short that time was.

“Alright.”

He lifted his head and looked at the other disciples.

“Let’s return first. The sect leader must be worried about us. Even if we have much to say, let’s postpone it for now.”

“Mm, okay.”

“Understood.”

The Mount Hua disciples also nodded obediently. They still had many questions, but this was the Southern land. It wasn’t a place to talk comfortably. They could talk more after leaving the Southern lands.

“Now then, first...”

“Wait a moment.”

“Uh?”

Chung Myung stopped abruptly and looked at the one who spoke. Un Geom stood calmly, looking at him.

“What is the matter, Un Geom senior sasuk?”

Before answering, Un Geom stared at the desolate Hangzhou land with a stern face.

“I worry whether it is right to say this to you in such a situation. So consider this not as an order from a superior, but just my opinion, and answer me.”

“Yes, senior sasuk. Please speak.”

“I feel uneasy about just leaving like this.”

“Yes...?”

Baek Cheon asked with a puzzled face. Un Geom answered bitterly.

“Haven’t too many died? Even those who had nothing to do with the murim world, just living their lives.”

A moment of silence passed. Everyone became solemn. They had been comforted by surviving successive crises and had momentarily forgotten what had happened here.

“I know the situation is difficult. Maybe it’s selfish of me to put you in danger again by bringing this up. But... if possible, I want to hold at least a simple requiem here for those who died unjustly. Would that be alright?”

Baek Cheon closed his eyes tightly. Sometimes he forgets that they are Taoists before warriors, and what must be done before winning.

‘Sasuk.’

While everyone else forgot, only Un Geom did not. Each time, Baek Cheon realized anew that he still had a long way to go to follow the masters.

“I think I’m fine...”

“We must!”

Before Baek Cheon finished, Chung Myung answered firmly.

“We were weak and insufficient to save the people here, but at least we must comfort their souls. That is the duty of one who has become a Taoist.”

Watching the situation as they prepared to leave, Im So-Byeong spoke with a worried face.

“Um...? This is the Southern land, and we still don’t know how the Four Sects Alliance will respond...”

“We must.”

“Damn it, stupid bastard. I forgot that. I would have kicked the ground if we had just left.”

“Exactly.”

“What should we prepare first? We should start quickly, I think.”

“The place first.”

“Hey? Can you hear me? Hey?”

The Mount Hua disciples, except Im So-Byeong, started bustling about. Even Hae Yeon raised her voice with a flushed face.

“I’ll help too, sasuks!”

“You don’t know the requiem rites done at the Taoist temple.”

“I’ll do anything that requires effort. But after it’s over, please let me chant at least one sutra for them!”

“That’s only natural.”

Watching the lively Justice faction members bustling and moving about, Im So-Byeong looked up at the sky with a hollow gaze.

‘Maybe Jang Ilso is a bit more sane than these guys?’

It was truly a difficult matter to judge.

Chapter 1082: Since It Is The Duty Of The One Who Is A Taoist. (2)

The soul-soothing ceremony was held modestly.

There were too few supplies prepared to hold a formal soul-soothing ceremony at the Taoist temple; they couldn’t even provide the most basic incense and incense burners.

Though they couldn’t fulfill all the formalities, the Mount Hua disciples chanting the prayer and reciting the Taoist scripture maintained an extremely reverent posture.

Ultimately, rituals are merely a courtesy to the dead. No matter how splendid the ceremony, it holds no greater value than a sincere nod.

The Mount Hua disciples knew this well, so they put their sincerity into every movement and every word they uttered, praying that those who died here would find peaceful rest even after death.

A fact that must never be forgotten: before being warriors, they were Taoists pursuing the Way.

But even the Mount Hua disciples sometimes forget who they truly are. This moment was both to honor the spirits of the victims and to reclaim their true duty.

Chung Myung, standing at the back, looked at the backs of the disciples reciting the Taoist scripture.

From Tang Soso on the far left to Baek Cheon on the far right, Chung Myung took in every one of their backs without missing a detail, then quietly closed his eyes.

‘Sahyung.’

In the past, Mount Hua also often held disciple initiation ceremonies. Chung Myung tried hard to avoid the tedious rituals, but Chung Mun sahyung always tried to catch him and make him stand even in a corner.

You are a Taoist before being a swordsman.

A sword without agreement is no different from a black mountain bandit’s blade. Because it is you, more than anyone else, you must not forget your duty as a Taoist.

You say you pursue the Way, but the foundation of soul-soothing is compassion to people. If you were not born with it, you must at least observe and learn. That is the first thing you must have as a Mount Hua disciple.

“...A cliché nagging.”

Yes, that was how he thought in the past. He wondered what meaning there was in the boring, repetitive rituals.

Once a person dies, that’s it. What use is the respect paid to those buried in the earth, and what value is there in the tears shed for them?

‘That was the case.’

Chung Myung slowly opened his closed eyes.

At that time, Chung Myung could not understand. He was not someone who ascended Mount Hua to learn the value of the Way, but someone abandoned by Mount Hua.

He grew up listening to the Taoist scripture like a lullaby and watching the Taoist rituals like play. So to him, all this naturally existed without needing to think about its meaning.

Therefore, he did not know.

Why people mourn those who can never return and honor their spirits.

Because those who had nothing from the start cannot lose anything.

‘Sahyung, I...’

What he lost was not something he held from the beginning but something he created while living. No... even though he did not want it, it simply stayed around him as if it were natural.

Therefore, he came to understand. Only after losing.

That even those who died and cannot return have meaning. And how those left behind come to the Taoist temple with what feelings.

And here are those who understand that meaning. Those who know what the old Chung Myung did not and try to protect what he could not.

The voices of the still young Mount Hua disciples solemnly reciting the Taoist scripture echoed clearly across the desolate land.

Un Geom, standing at the front, placed his remaining hand over his chest in salute. Each of his movements was filled with deep sorrow for those who died here.

As he began reciting the Taoist scripture again, the Mount Hua disciples followed. Chung Myung, standing at the back watching them, slowly closed his eyes. The scripture flowed from his lips.

It was nothing particularly special or remarkable. But anyone who knows who Chung Myung is would not see this scene as just that.

Chung Myung had participated in countless soul-soothing ceremonies but had never recited the Taoist scripture with his own mouth. Though he could recite it with his eyes closed from hearing it so often, never once had he done so.

He felt embarrassed and awkward. And he thought it did not suit him.

But at this moment, Chung Myung recited the scripture with sincerity. Not just knowing the words, but speaking with a heart full of hope and longing.

This was a prayer for the dead and comfort for those left behind.

And perhaps a reaching hand toward those who cannot stay anywhere.

After Un Geom finished the scripture, he stepped back. Then Hae Yeon, who had been waiting behind, cautiously stepped forward. After a solemn bow, he slowly began chanting the Buddhist sutra.

They believed in different things. They pursued different paths. But at this moment, their hearts are surely the same.

As Hae Yeon’s clear chanting spread widely, the Mount Hua disciples maintained their posture without faltering. Looking at the devastated Hangzhou, an indescribable sadness welled up in their eyes.

Baek Cheon lightly clenched his fist, so the emotions swelling in his chest would not scatter, and so he would not forget.

Jo Gul, Yoon Jong, Yu Yiseol, and Tang Soso also wore similar expressions.

Finally, Hae Yeon’s long recitation ended. He bowed deeply and nodded his head. With a sorrowful face, he turned and stood again beside the Mount Hua disciples.

Baek Cheon naturally looked at Un Geom. Un Geom smiled gently, as if to say this was as far as he would lead.

At that moment, a deep sigh escaped Baek Cheon’s lips.

‘I...’

His fist clenched tightly.

‘I couldn’t save anyone.’

When they came here, their goal was to stop the Demonic Cult and save those still alive here. But before they knew it, all their focus was only on survival.

They survived, rejoiced, and felt relief from what they did.

But now they know. That it should not have been so.

Because what they failed to protect here was far too great to be happy just for their own safety.

He thought about stepping forward but stopped in place. Now was not the time to preach to them. It was time to speak standing alongside them.

A calm voice came from Baek Cheon’s mouth.

“...If the sect leader were here... he would have shed tears for those who died first more than anyone else.”

The Mount Hua disciples lowered their heads.

“If the elders were here, they would not have forgotten the victims throughout the fight with the enemies.”

Because the voice was calm, it sounded even a little sad.

“The reason we couldn’t do so is because we are weak.”

The place left by pride, relief, and childish feelings was filled with regret.

“Not because we lack strength, but because people are weak. We say we are Taoists, but we do not pursue the Way; we say we care for others, but we do not truly look at others.”

Everyone slowly nodded at those painful words.

“The strength the sect leader wants from us... is not the power to subdue others. It is the strength to look back on those weaker than myself even when my own life is in danger... yes.”

Baek Cheon’s voice was slow but firm.

“It is probably the strength of being human.”

He paused for a moment and quietly closed his eyes.

They fought their best and defeated the enemy. Yet the reason their hearts felt so empty was because there are things strength alone cannot fill.

“We will have to endure many things ahead.”

Everyone looked at the devastated land of Hangzhou.

“Next time... yes, next time, let’s never forget. What we fight for. Why we must fight.”

Un Geom bowed his head and shouted the Taoist call. As if it were a signal, the Mount Hua disciples all shouted the Taoist call with their internal qi, spreading it across the vast land of Hangzhou.

In response, Hae Yeon also loudly shouted the Buddhist chant.

A Buddhist chant is a self-prayer; a Taoist call is a self-wish.

It is a cry for salvation. Also, like a candle lit so that weak humans who forget what they must not forget in life do not lose their way.

Their Taoist call was louder and clearer than ever.

And as loud as that call was, their bowed heads naturally felt heavier than ever.

A moment of silence passed.

They gained much, but in truth, gained nothing. Only after calming down did they see the true nature of this tragedy, which was hard to even call a war.

Then, a quiet voice of Chung Myung was heard in their ears.

“...Let’s go back.”

“Yes...”

Everyone slowly turned their steps. It was a place they wanted to flee from just moments ago, but strangely, their feet would not move. Probably because of lingering regrets over what they failed to protect.

“Next time...”

He tried to speak calmly, but Chung Myung’s words ended with lingering regret. Looking at the ruins before him, he finally turned his head away.

“Next time, we just have to protect it.”

“...Yes.”

Baek Cheon’s voice, filled with emptiness, echoed hollowly.

“That’s how it should be.”

As he tried to gather his heavy heart and lighten the mood by patting his disciples’ shoulders, it happened.

‘Huh?’

His brow furrowed slightly. Even as everyone turned around, Yu Yiseol, who stood looking ahead until the last moment, suddenly widened her eyes.

‘Enemy?’

Startled, Baek Cheon hurriedly turned his head.

But there was nothing in particular to see. Only the still desolate...

“Huh?”

“N-now?”

At that moment, surprised voices burst from the Mount Hua disciples’ mouths. They immediately shut their mouths and held their breath with stiff faces.

At that moment.

“Ah...!”

Everyone heard it.

A faint voice, as if a hallucination, but unmistakably someone’s weak voice.

Without thinking, their feet moved first. When they came to their senses, they were already running at full speed.

And the back of someone who looked desperate beyond urgency ran ahead, forward and forward.

Chung Myung pushed off the ground and dashed at full speed. Then he began frantically digging through the building debris. His wounded hands trembled.

The Mount Hua disciples who arrived a bit later also immediately rushed in and dug through the wreckage with their bare hands.

Towards the faint breathing sounds coming from within. That faint warmth they had only just found, though it was surely here.

“Ugh! Ugh...!”

A suppressed groan burst from Chung Myung’s urgent mouth. His hands, which had been roughly digging through the debris, began to move more carefully.

Finally, Chung Myung’s hands stopped.

“... Me ...”

The voice, faint enough to be drowned out by the wind, was finally heard clearly.

“Help... me...”

Crack!

The debris in Chung Myung’s hand crumbled. To avoid injuring the buried person, he began using his internal qi to pulverize the rubble.

How many times did he repeat this?

After an unprecedentedly careful touch, finally a person appeared beneath the rubble.

No, it was not just one person.

“Here, here!”

“There’s a baby too... a baby!”

A dust-covered woman, half-conscious, and a small child cradled in her arms were seen.

The woman, completely exhausted and seemingly unable to open her eyes, kept whispering a voice that seemed about to break off but never did.

“...Help... me...”

“...”

“My... baby...”

No one dared to speak.

In the silence, Chung Myung stretched his trembling hand into the rubble. Holding the woman’s pulse very carefully, he began to push his vital energy into her. The same for the child.

“Alive...”

At that moment, everyone saw Chung Myung’s shoulders collapse. The man of Kangho could no longer hold on after discovering the two survivors and dropped his head.

“Alive... they are...”

Baek Cheon, staring blankly at the bowed Chung Myung, the unconscious woman, and the sleeping child in her arms, bit his lip tightly and looked up at the distant sky.

She must have held her breath, afraid of the sound of clashing swords. Afraid that the noise of fighting and crashing would take the child away.

Even in the pitch-black darkness, she must have covered her mouth and endured, fearing a scream might escape.

What made that woman muster her last strength was neither the power that defeated the Demonic Cult, nor the sword that pierced the high priest’s throat, nor the mind that threatened and drove away the Four Sect’s leader.

It was simply the resounding Taoist call. The sorrowful Buddhist chant. A voice without any mighty power, just a voice earnestly hoping and hoping again.

Therefore... it was conveyed to those who were wounded and trembling with fear.

What ultimately saved those who could not be saved even by the power to uplift the world... was the small mindset they had momentarily forgotten but must never forget.

Tears welled up in Baek Cheon’s eyes.

The blue sky was unusually piercing, painful to the eyes.

Chapter 1083: Since It Is The Duty Of The One Who Is A Taoist. (3)

Whoosh.

The disciples of Mount Hua dashed out like the wind. Yet, they kept glancing at the child cradled in Tang Soso’s arms.

“...Are they really okay?”

“I said they’re fine.”

“...Are you really sure?”

“I told you, they’re fine!”

“So really...”

“You, huh?”

Tang Soso, who had been patient all along, finally flared up, and Baek Cheon winced.

“Ah, no. I was just worried...”

Seeing Baek Cheon act unusually like a fool, she shook her head.

“The child is fine. Just exhausted. Rather, that person is the problem.”

At those words, everyone’s gaze snapped back to the woman carried on Yu Yiseol’s back.

“If we had found her just a little later, it would have been truly irreversible.”

“Ah...”

At those words, everyone shuddered.

Had they simply left Hangzhou, this woman and child would have been buried in the debris and lost their lives. In such an exhausted state, they could not have escaped alone.

“Nothing bad will happen, right...?”

“No, absolutely not!”

“Please shut your mouth, Taoist!”

“Enough!”

“...Sorry, but who was that last guy?”

Baek Cheon glared fiercely, and Jo Gul quietly turned his head. Baek Cheon gritted his teeth but held back, as there was no time to waste hitting someone like Jo Gul now.

First, they had to move that child to a safe place as quickly as possible.

“What about the Yangtze River?”

Im So-Byeong, following behind, answered in a slightly urgent tone.

“If we run north, it takes a full day.”

Then Baek Cheon’s gaze turned to the woman carried by Yu Yiseol. Since Tang Soso said she was fine, no immediate danger was expected, but he still felt uneasy.

Perhaps the urgency he felt was not rational. It might be the anxiety of never wanting to lose what they barely saved from the dreadful Hangzhou.

But Baek Cheon knows that sometimes something more important than reason exists. The anxiety they feel now is not wrong.

“Sasuk!”

“Yes. Let’s increase the speed.”

“W-wait! Taoists! Faster from here?”

Im So-Byeong shouted with a face that said, ‘Hey, you crazy bastards! Are you serious right now?’ but Baek Cheon only glanced elsewhere without answering.

Im So-Byeong naturally followed Baek Cheon’s gaze. Namgung Do-Wei’s face was pale blue, clearly saying, ‘Even if I die running, I will never slow down!’

“...So this is why Father told me not to associate with those Justice faction bastards...”

Who said the Evil Sect guys were crazy? Those who say that should all be caught and thrown to that crazy Mount Hua sect.

“Run!”

“Eek! C-come with me!”

Im So-Byeong was terrified but followed the speeding disciples of Mount Hua.

For others, it might be just keeping pace, but for Im So-Byeong, it was a matter of survival. If he were left behind alone in this Jiangnan land, who knows what would happen next.

“Ah, no. C-come with me... cough I’m sick too... cough Am I not sick? You bastards! cough cough cough!”

Im So-Byeong, his insides churning, coughed as if about to spit out his lungs, but surprisingly, no one paid him any attention.

Actually, one person did glance his way. Perhaps out of a bit more affection than others, Chung Myung looked back with slightly pitying eyes.

“What!”

It was the moment Im So-Byeong was about to shout with a face full of emotion.

“Tsk tsk. That Evil Sect bastard. I wondered when he’d die, and looks like it’s finally now.”

“...”

“Whether you die here alone or while running, death is death. Might as well get buried early, right? Isn’t that the way to leave a whole corpse?”

“...You bastard...”

“What?”

“cough cough cough”

Im So-Byeong quickly coughed and avoided Chung Myung’s gaze. Of course, he cursed inwardly.

‘Did I say something wrong, you bastard?’

Those bastards talk about compassion. The Grand Elder might get so mad he’d knee them in the head.

“Ugh...”

At that moment, a low moan escaped from the woman being carried on Yu Yiseol’s back. Everyone stopped talking and looked back at her. It wasn’t a moan of regaining consciousness, but just a sound that slipped out.

“Samae, are you okay? Shall we switch?”

“I’ll do it.”

“...Alright.”

Baek Cheon quietly nodded.

For a woman of the murim world, it might be nothing, but for a private person like a woman, being carried on a strange person’s back could be a big deal. So, among them, the woman, Yu Yiseol had no choice but to carry her.

‘It must be hard.’

Baek Cheon looked at Yu Yiseol with pity.

Running while carrying a patient is harder than one thinks. Any shaking can worsen the patient’s condition, so even stepping carefully requires great attention. It consumes twice the usual stamina and internal qi.

But Yu Yiseol ran carrying the woman without a single complaint. Responsibility deeply seeped from her expressionless face.

“Soso, shall I hold the child?”

“Take your hands off, sahyung! Don’t put those dirty hands on the child!”

“...Dirty, huh.”

In a corner, Jo Gul was deeply hurt by those words. But Tang Soso absolutely refused to hand over the child and bared her teeth.

“Anyone else is fine, but not sahyung.”

“That’s right.”

“You go back there. If the child wakes and sees your face, the child will have a seizure.”

“...Seriously these guys...”

The disciples of Mount Hua pushed forward quickly. Though joking to shake off the tension of a possible Evil Sect Alliance attack, their movements were desperate.

“Huff... huff!”

As time passed, Im So-Byeong, who had kept up well, began to lag behind. His chronic illness inevitably affected his stamina. Besides, he hadn’t stopped pushing himself since departure.

The disciples of Mount Hua tried to glance back for a moment, but a calm voice pierced their ears.

“Just run.”

After saying that, Chung Myung slipped back. Then, with a displeased expression, he placed his hand on Im So-Byeong’s back.

“What kind of bandit has such weak stamina?”

“W-why would a bandit run like this... cough What is there!”

“Yeah, yeah.”

“S-still, huff! I am helping...”

Chung Myung clicked his tongue lightly.

“Well, you earned your keep, at least.”

If not for Im So-Byeong, they would have all died when the Black Ghost Fortress attacked. He never thought he’d say he owed his life to an Evil Sect guy.

“Run faster, bandit bastard. Before I really leave you behind.”

“...You scoundrel.”

“Huh?”

“...Nothing.”

Im So-Byeong pouted and pushed strength into his legs. The hat on his head was crumpled and slipped down. Still, thanks to Chung Myung pushing his back, he regained some composure.

Then suddenly, he slowed down again. Chung Myung asked curiously.

“What are you doing, bastard?”

“Wait a moment.”

Im So-Byeong, having put some distance from the others, spoke in a low voice.

“Isn’t it a bit strange, Taoist?”

“What is?”

“That woman and child.”

Looking at the woman carried on Yu Yiseol’s back, Im So-Byeong narrowed his eyes.

“We searched just in case, but there were no other survivors around.”

“...”

“Even strong men couldn’t endure it, so how could a woman like that survive the collision... It’s just...”

Chung Myung didn’t answer and kept running. Seeing his grim expression, Im So-Byeong didn’t press for an answer and ran silently. After a long while, he spoke again, his voice very heavy.

“Am I right in my guess?”

“...I don’t know.”

Im So-Byeong cast a meaningful glance at the woman and child. The one who unleashed demonic qi to devastate the area was Dan Ja-Kang. Jang Ilso and Chung Myung attacked only Dan Ja-Kang.

Since Dan Ja-Kang was the cause of the deaths of those swept away, the reason that child and woman survived must also be found in Dan Ja-Kang.

But... Im So-Byeong soon shook his head. This was a battlefield where lives were at stake. Is it really possible for someone consumed by demonic qi and raging to avoid harming a specific place?

“It might just be a coincidence.”

“...That’s true.”

Neither Chung Myung nor Im So-Byeong said more. Perhaps the answer would never be known. The only one who could answer was no longer in this world.

Chung Myung’s eyes, watching ahead, darkened.

This was a question even he couldn’t answer. Chung Myung didn’t know the woman and child were there. Facing foes like Dan Ja-Kang and Heaven Killer, he wasn’t strong enough to divide his attention elsewhere.

As Im So-Byeong said, all this might just be coincidence.

But if... just one in a thousand, one in ten thousand, what Chung Myung thinks is right...

‘Even if they’re from the Demonic cult... in the end, they’re still human children.’

It’s something he doesn’t want to think about. Even if he knows, he must forget.

“So... it’s something that must be done.”

At Chung Myung’s muttering, Im So-Byeong gave a confused look. But Chung Myung didn’t look at him. His mind was already filled with another presence.

‘Heavenly Demon.’

No matter how much doctrine one accepts, or how much brainwashing one undergoes, humans ultimately doubt and doubt again. And they cannot abandon the small compassion in their hearts.

That is why the Heavenly Demon is so terrible.

Because it turns such humans into blind fanatics.

‘Definitely... even if I have to stake everything.’

Never again should a world where the Heavenly Demon runs rampant be allowed. To prevent the tragedy of Hangzhou from repeating.

With resolve, Chung Myung bit his lips tightly and pushed Im So-Byeong strongly.

“S-say it in words!”

After running again until dusk, the disciples of Mount Hua finally arrived at the Yangtze River.

Exhausted beyond words, they barely arrived and saw a huge ship standing by the riverbank. Its affiliation was unmistakably clear.

Speechless, staring blankly at the ship, they looked at each other.

“That’s the Water pirate bastards’ ship, right?”

“...Seems like it.”

“Looks like no one’s inside?”

“Why is this here then...?”

Among those who couldn’t hide their dismay, Chung Myung walked out and chuckled at the empty ship.

“Anyway, what a nasty personality, that Jang Ilso bastard.”

Yoon Jong looked at the empty ship in disbelief and said.

“...When did he even prepare this?”

“Who knows. Does it matter?”

Chung Myung gestured toward the ship.

“Looks like we’re supposed to board it. Let’s go.”

“T-this?”

“It’s the Water Pirate bastards’ ship.”

“Then what? Swim with these people?”

The disciples of Mount Hua looked at the ship reluctantly but soon nodded.

“...No choice. We have to board.”

“Yes, senior sasuk.”

Even Un Geom said so, so the disciples of Mount Hua, though uneasy, boarded the empty ship. Raising the anchor and unfolding the folded sail, the ship slowly began moving along the river.

“...It is far.”

“Indeed.”

Leaning weakly against the railing, the disciples of Mount Hua looked at the slowly receding Jiangnan with eyes full of complex emotions. Though less than two days had passed, it felt like months.

As they silently watched the fading land, a child’s cry brushed past their ears.

“...Looks like the child woke up.”

Tang Soso began soothing the child in her arms. The child’s small hand tightly gripped her thumb. Seeing this, everyone smiled faintly.

Baek Cheon, unable to take his eyes off the scene, spoke.

“Next time...”

“Yes, sasuk.”

Yoon Jong answered calmly.

“Next time will be different.”

They looked at each other, then silently gazed at the child cradled in Tang Soso’s arms again.

A ship quietly sailed over the river dyed red by the sunset.

Chapter 1084: Since It Is The Duty Of The One Who Is A Taoist. (4)

Her throat was parched, as if something rough was scraping the inside. Her body burned like a fireball, sinking deeper into an endless darkness.

As she descended, a strange sensation approached—cool yet warm, gentle.

“Ah...”

The woman slowly opened her eyes. As she barely lifted her heavy eyelids, she saw a dim ceiling and someone holding her hand.

“Who...”

She saw a pale-faced young man.

Even in her hazy state, a flicker of alertness stirred. But the moment she noticed the black Taoist robe he wore, her tension eased.

“Taoist...”

She vaguely recalled the sound of a Taoist prayer she had heard while her consciousness blurred.

“Ah...”

As she opened her mouth to speak, a clear voice pierced her ear. A young female Taoist with braided pigtails spoke.

“Are you awake?”

It was strange. Though she was definitely seeing this female Taoist for the first time, the sight of that Taoist robe somehow reassured her. After all, no one wearing a Taoist robe would harm civilians.

“How is she feeling?”

When the female Taoist asked, the man holding her pulse replied,

“Much more stable now.”

“Then step back and rest. Sahyung’s body isn’t in good shape either.”

“I’m fine.”

“...Seriously, he’s stubborn and won’t listen, even when I say he doesn’t have to do it.”

“Still okay. I can endure it.”

“Yes, yes. Do as you please. But I’ll report this to the sect leader and elders.”

The woman, listening absentmindedly to their bickering, suddenly shuddered as she recalled something.

“Ha, Haki... my Haki...!”

“Oh my. Look at me!”

The pigtail-haired woman ran to one side, then returned holding something wrapped in a thick blanket.

“Your child is fine.”

The woman took the child with trembling hands. Only after confirming the peaceful face visible through the swaddling did calmness settle over her.

“Ah...”

Her hand gently stroked the child’s cheek.

“The child fell asleep from exhaustion. There’s no health problem, so please don’t worry. The child woke earlier and cried loudly once.”

“Thank you... truly thank you...”

“Not at all.”

Filled with relief and emotion, the woman was about to speak when suddenly the door burst open with a thud.

“You’re awake?”

Dark-clothed men rushed inside. Startled, the woman looked at those running in.

They were also Taoists wearing black robes. Despite the swords at their waists, they didn’t feel very threatening—likely because of their clothes and the worry and concern on their faces.

“Be quiet, you lot! There’s a patient here!”

“...Yes.”

“Sorry...”

“We just got a bit excited...”

When the pigtail-haired woman shouted sharply, the men who had been running in cheerfully all dropped their shoulders and bowed deeply. Their crumpled appearance reassured the woman.

“But this place is...”

“Ah, this is inside a boat.”

“A boat...?”

The pigtail-haired woman, Tang Soso, smiled and replied,

“Yes. Hangzhou no longer seems livable, so we took her in first. We’re heading north of the river now.”

The woman momentarily stiffened and stared at the ceiling.

The moment she heard “Hangzhou,” hellish scenes flashed vividly in her mind. Overwhelmed by fear, her body stiffened and curled up. Then, the Taoist beside her held her hand, and a cool yet gentle qi surged through her.

The qi flowed through her body, pushing the fear away once more.

“Ah... I see.”

The woman didn’t ask further. How Hangzhou had fared, what had happened to others—she already knew all of that.

“You all are...”

“We are Taoists of the Mount Hua sect.”

Baek Cheon, standing just behind Tang Soso, spoke slowly, trying not to sound threatening.

“Mount Hua...”

“Yes. It’s a Taoist sect in Shaanxi.”

Baek Cheon glanced at his disciples beside him and spoke calmly.

“Though we look like this, surprisingly, we are Taoists. We are not bad people, so don’t worry.”

“...Is it right to judge by appearances?”

“Gul.”

“What? Sahyung, aren’t you offended with...”

“It’s okay for Sasuk though.”

For a moment, Jo Gul was speechless and glanced up at Baek Cheon’s face. Then, with a dark expression, he muttered something unintelligible, probably complaining about the filthy world and its inequalities.

“You don’t have to worry anymore. The Demonic Cult that attacked Hangzhou has all retreated.”

“...You saved me...”

“You were lucky.”

Hearing the calm voice, the woman was silent for a moment. Then tears welled up in her eyes.

“...Thank you.”

As her trembling voice escaped, everyone held their breath.

“Thank... you. Truly... truly thank you...”

The disciples of Mount Hua, unsure what to say, silently looked at the woman. Then, Un Geom, watching from behind, smiled gently and spoke.

“We only did what any Taoist should do. We apologize for being late.”

“No... Thank you.”

Jo Geol, wiping his nose awkwardly, changed the subject while looking at the child in the woman’s arms.

“That kid looks really smart. Reminds me of myself as a child.”

Criticism erupted from all sides.

“How dare you speak rudely to a child!”

“Is that something a Taoist should say? You despicable scoundrel!”

“Shut your mouth!”

Though Jo Gul, a wealthy child raised with care, looked very aggrieved, unfortunately Mount Hua was a ruthless sect that didn’t care about individual grievances.

“No, look! This cheek here...”

Jo Gul pressed the child’s cheek with his index finger. At that touch, the child opened its eyes and suddenly burst into tears.

“Uh, uh? I-I didn’t mean that...”

“But this brat really!”

In the end, Yoon Jong’s fist mercilessly struck Jo Gul’s jaw. Jo Gul, fearing to startle the child, couldn’t even scream and collapsed right there.

“Looks like the child is hungry.”

“Ah...”

The child’s mother looked at the crying child, then turned her gaze to the disciples of Mount Hua.

“Can we help with something?”

“What should we do?”

“Just tell us!”

Everyone panicked and blurted out whatever came to mind. Tang Soso’s forehead veins bulged.

“Sahyungs.”

“Yes?”

“...The child needs to be breastfed. Please leave immediately.”

“Yes.”

The men of Mount Hua rushed out without looking back. Chung Myung also got up and left with them.

“Anyway...”

Tang Soso clicked her tongue, shook her head, and firmly closed the open door. Meanwhile, the woman nursing the child slowly stroked her child’s cheek and head. Tang Soso spoke in a surprisingly gentle voice.

“We’ll prepare some rice gruel soon, so please wait a little.”

“I can’t trouble you so much...”

“Trouble? It’s only natural for us to do this. Don’t worry. Our sahyungs may be a bit foolish, but they’re capable people.”

Tang Soso added with a slight smile.

“Everyone here is kind too. They’re just a bit too good-hearted...”

The woman silently looked at Tang Soso. Tang Soso’s gaze toward the walking people was warm. It clearly showed how much she cared for and liked them.

‘This is a good place.’

Just from that relationship, she felt that everyone here must be warm-hearted.

Then the woman hesitantly spoke with a suddenly darkened face.

“Where should I go now...”

“Huh? Ah!”

Tang Soso turned to the woman and suddenly remembered, then explained,

“Hangzhou has become too dangerous to live in, so we plan to take you somewhere safe. Do you have anywhere to rely on?”

The woman shook her head with a helpless and sorrowful expression.

Those who cultivate the land usually live with their families in one area as a cluster. If she can’t return to Hangzhou, it means she and her child have nowhere to settle in this vast Central Plains.

“Hmm. Then... there’s the option of relying on a village by the Yangtze River...”

Tang Soso scratched her cheek as she spoke. Raising a child alone in a foreign place is a very difficult and dangerous task.

“If you have nowhere to go, you could go to Sichuan or Shaanxi. In Sichuan, the Tang family is building a new village, and Shaanxi’s Hua-Um is a very good place to live with kind people.”

The woman’s face hardened. Sichuan and Shaanxi—she had only heard the names, not knowing where they were. The thought of going there alone to raise a child filled her with sudden worry.

But at that moment, someone tightly held her hand.

Turning her head, she saw a woman who had been there unnoticed, holding her hand with an indifferent expression. A calm voice flowed out.

“It’s okay.”

“...”

“Because they’re all good places.”

An expressionless face. Though her features were cold and somewhat frightening, the warmth of her hand strangely brought reassurance.

Yu Yiseol, not used to dealing with people like this, desperately tried to reassure the woman.

Tang Soso saw Yu Yiseol and smiled gently, then cheerfully added,

“Yes, that’s right. Don’t worry too much. Our sect leader will handle those things somehow. Just focus on recovering your body.”

“...Yes.”

The woman holding the child clenched her hand.

The clenched hand seemed to say she would protect her child no matter what. Yu Yiseol released her hand and stood up.

Tang Soso spoke.

“Then rest a bit. We’ll bring rice gruel soon.”

“...Thank you.”

She quietly left the cabin with Yu Yiseol. As soon as the door closed, Yu Yiseol asked Tang Soso,

“Is it really okay?”

“Yes. Thanks to Chung Myung sahyung pushing himself too hard.”

“...”

“Anyway, he’s unstoppable. Even though his body isn’t well, he can’t help but nag.”

“Because he’s that kind of person. Always.”

“Yes, he is. I know that, but...”

Tang Soso sighed deeply. She understood the worry but hoped others would realize that he too was someone to be worried about. Though it wouldn’t be easy.

“The child...”

“The child is fine too, Sago. Don’t worry.”

Yu Yiseol nodded slightly and stared at the rippling Yangtze River. After a while, she spoke softly.

“My mother too...”

“...”

“Did my mother look at me with eyes like that?”

Tang Soso quietly reached out and gently held Yu Yiseol’s hand.

“She probably did.”

“...”

“I am certain of it.”

Yu Yiseol slowly nodded. After a moment, she asked,

“How do you cook rice gruel?”

“...I’ll do it.”

“I asked how to cook it.”

“I’ll do it.”

At that soft but firm answer, Yu Yiseol pouted slightly.

Chapter 1085: Since It Is The Duty Of The One Who Is A Taoist. (5)

“What...”

A soulless voice escaped from Jo Gul’s mouth as he weakly stirred the ladle.

His eyes rolled back.

The disciples of Mount Hua—no, at this moment, they should be called useless surplus humans—swarmed around, watching him make porridge.

“Wow, you’re doing so well.”

“So Gul has something he’s good at.”

“You shouldn’t speak so carelessly, patrons. Everything in the world has some use, even if small. Amitabha.”

Damn these people.

Jo Gul gritted his teeth and glared at Tang Soso.

“What?”

“...Nothing.”

“What?”

It’s not entirely wrong.

Tang Soso could now become a proud bandit leader just by joining any mountain bandit troop, but in the past, she was the precious jewel of the Sichuan Tang family. Even Jo Gul, who was raised somewhat nobly, couldn’t compare his noble status to hers.

So it’s possible she never made porridge before. That could be, but...

“...Even so, can you burn porridge? Is that even possible? For a person to do that?”

Jo Gul shouted sharply at Un Geom, who nodded as if impressed. Hey, why are you nodding there!

Baek Cheon and Tang Soso, officially recognized as children of noble families by Mount Hua(?), quietly avoided Jo Gul’s gaze.

Yoon Jong, Yu Yiseol, Chung Myung—the self-proclaimed Mount Hua street trio—looked sullenly at Jo Gul. Yoon Jong was the only one showing a hint of apology.

“Sorry. I have no talent for cooking.”

“Me?”

Yu Yiseol pointed to her face and tilted her head.

“None.”

“What?”

“Kitchen.”

“...”

So stop it. My heart feels like it’s breaking...

“Enough, just stir the porridge quickly. But does porridge usually take this long?”

“No...”

Jo Gul sighed as he looked at the huge pot in front of him.

‘Is this food for people? It’s for cows.’

They said just one bowl is enough, but everyone rushed in asking if that fills the stomach, and what if they want more, shouldn’t we cook a lot in advance, are we saving grain from the patients now...!

Adding grain bit by bit, the huge pot was filled to bursting with porridge.

‘If we make a pot like this, even the largest physician hall in Luoyang couldn’t finish it in a day.’

But what can be done? If you’re upset, you should have joined earlier. As he sighed stirring the porridge, Jo Gul’s eyebrows twitched slightly.

“The fire seems a bit weak...”

“Really?”

Creak.

At that moment, Chung Myung casually tore off the ship’s railing, then snapped it into pieces and threw them into the fire beneath the pot.

“More?”

“No. The fire is fine now, but, um... Chung Myung.”

“Why?”

“...Is it really okay to do this?”

Chung Myung showed the wooden debris left in his hand, which was just called the railing moments ago.

Hae Yeon smiled warmly upon hearing those words.

...It seems that guy has completely lost it now.

Chung Myung looked at Jo Gul with a bewildered expression.

At this point, Jo Gul gave up on further conversation. Arguing logic with this guy was worse than playing GO with Baek Ah.

Pushing everyone aside, Jo Gul fiercely stirred the porridge, then after a while put down the ladle and took the pot off the fire.

“...It’s done.”

“I’m sure.”

The street trio, who held a firm and clear grudge against the rich, looked at Jo Gul with distrust.

“It’s unsettling that a rich kid is making porridge.”

“Jo Gul.”

Ah, I just want everything to die already. Really.

Then Tang Soso quietly tasted the porridge and nodded.

“This is good enough.”

“Really?”

“Ooooh.”

“Finally, Jo Gul’s usefulness has been found. It took a long time though.”

Namgung Do-Wei, unable to approach the mess, stood a bit away watching the scene, then looked at Im So-Byeong beside him with trembling eyes.

Meanwhile, Tang Soso busily transferred the porridge into prepared bowls and quickly headed to the cabin. Yu Yiseol followed her briskly.

Before Tang Soso closed the cabin door, she turned her head back and said,

As the cabin door closed, everyone’s gaze turned simultaneously to the pot on the floor.

“That’s right. We haven’t eaten anything.”

“...Haven’t we starved for three days?”

“I never thought of that.”

A moment of silence passed.

Growl.

Breaking the silence, a stomach growl came from someone, and everyone glanced at each other before slowly approaching the pot.

Click.

Baek Cheon dipped his spoon into the porridge bowl and chuckled at something.

“...Really, there’s nothing missing—pot, bowls, everything. It feels like they brought the very ship the Pirates were on until recently.”

“There was even rice onboard. I didn’t expect them to bring cooked rice too.”

“...I wouldn’t have eaten it if it weren’t for Soso.”

“Me too. How can I trust that bastard?”

If there’s someone who can check for poison, at least you don’t worry about dying from food.

It doesn’t seem like the rice was deliberately prepared; it looks like the Pirates carried it as emergency rations. But the important thing is that there was grain to fill the stomach.

Baek Cheon glanced outside the ship. Their ship was hugging the right side of the Yangtze River, heading upstream, so they could quickly disembark to the north bank if the Pirates attacked.

At first, they thought to use the ship only for crossing and then travel safely on land, but that wasn’t feasible with children and patients.

So they found a compromise between more comfortable and safer travel.

Fortunately or unfortunately, they hadn’t seen any other Pirate ships on the way here, only passing by some docked ones.

Though unlikely, it was suspicious enough to wonder if Jang Ilso had tied up all the Pirates to let them pass comfortably.

While Baek Cheon spoke, the porridge in the pot kept decreasing. His gaze grew urgent. He too hadn’t eaten for three days.

Baek Cheon’s speech quickened.

“So let’s be more vigilant. If it were just us, maybe not, but here...”

Even Un Geom, Im So-Byeong, and Namgung Do-Wei quickly emptied their bowls. Eventually, Baek Cheon gave up lecturing and rushed to the pot.

“Leave some food for me, you damn bastards!”

The deck was instantly filled with eating sounds and spoon movements. It was truly a battle-like meal.

“Yes, sect leader.”

Hyun Jong sighed deeply. His gaze never left the river and the land beyond in the south.

“Even now, crossing the river...”

“...I suppose so.”

Hyun Jong sighed heavily.

If they crossed clumsily and the situation got complicated, they might not contact in time and the kids could be harmed. Because of this, Hyun Jong and the rest of Mount Hua disciples were stuck here, helplessly waiting.

“Let’s go a bit further downstream.”

“We’ve already come far, sect leader.”

“I know, I know. But shouldn’t we consider the possibility they can’t contact us? We should go where we can reach them fastest if trouble arises.”

“...That’s true, but...”

Un Am sighed deeply.

They had already moved far west from the river mouth. It was a result of wanting to hold position and also to reach the kids quickly if something happened.

Click.

Hyun Jong finally started biting his nails. Seeing the worry and anxiety on his face, Un Am couldn’t say no.

“Then just a little...”

“Sect leader! A Pirate ship just moved ahead!”

“Let’s head downstream!”

As the Mount Hua disciples steeled themselves for a desperate fight and started running downstream at full speed.

“By the way, why is the fire still burning?”

“It’s warm.”

“That’s true.”

The Mount Hua disciples sat huddled around the campfire lit on the deck.

With faces like cats sitting in front of a fire.

Chapter 1086: What Sin Did I Commit In My Previous Life? (1)

“They’re already back?”

“I told you!”

“Y-You mean they’ve already defeated the Demonic Cult? It’s been less than four days since you left?”

Tang Su-Myeong asked again, his expression dumbfounded.

The fear inspired by the name Demonic Cult was deeply ingrained in their bones. Who didn’t know about the brutal war a hundred years ago? Every murim warrior felt the same.

Though it was tacitly forbidden even to mention it, it was no exaggeration to say that no murim warrior was unaware that the Demonic Cult was a terrifying and dreadful enemy.

So how could anyone not be shocked that they defeated such foes in just four days and returned?

“Is that even possible?”

“Come on, surely it wasn’t the entire Demonic Cult that came. It must have been just an advance party.”

“No, you fool. Do you think I don’t know that? Even if it’s an advance party, it’s still the Demonic Cult. Don’t you know what kind of place that is?”

“...True.”

Hearing this, Tang Moon-hyuk nodded. Even if it was just an advance party, it was still the Demonic Cult. Moreover, considering that this advance party devastated the entire Hangzhou, it couldn’t simply be dismissed as a mere advance party.

“If it were any less, that scoundrel, the Evil Sect leader, wouldn’t have begged for help. Rumor has it that the Ten Thousand People Clan suffered greatly at the hands of the Demonic Cult.”

“Surely it is not that bad...”

“Come on, do you think I’d make this up? This news came straight from the Beggars’ Union.”

“Is that true?”

“Of course!”

Tang Su-Myeong said firmly and then slowly shook his head.

“But... even if they borrowed the Evil Sect’s power, to finish off those bastards in just four days and come back... what exactly is this Mount Hua’s Sword Saint...?”

“Is it only the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s power? The Five Swords and other Mount Hua members who went along also contributed.”

“That’s right, that’s right. That makes sense.”

A subtle pride blossomed on the faces of the Tang family members talking.

Murim warriors usually didn’t enjoy seeing other sects gain prominence because it meant their own sect’s influence diminished relatively.

But on the faces of the Tang family members discussing Mount Hua’s achievements, there was no trace of displeasure.

“Indeed, the family lord’s foresight is truly remarkable.”

“Exactly. Who would have imagined Mount Hua would come this far?”

“Not only the elders but most people opposed it. Honestly, who would have thought of allying with Mount Hua back then? We are the great Tang family, and they were a ruined sect.”

Everyone expressed admiration in unison.

When Mount Hua’s status was far lower than now, Tang Gunak formed an equal alliance with them. The Tang family’s head had strong authority, and the elder council that actively opposed it was disbanded, so no one openly resisted. But inwardly, there was much dissatisfaction.

But who would have thought that choice would turn out like this.

“At that time, the only one in the world who truly understood Mount Hua’s true value was our family lord.”

“Exactly! That is why he must be our family lord.”

Pride naturally blossomed on the faces of the Tang Family members.

To the Tang Family, Mount Hua was not just another sect or outsider. They had maintained relations and supported Mount Hua since before it was recognized by the world, and they were their only blood alliance.

The higher Mount Hua’s status rose, the more the greatness of the Tang Family was highlighted, so there was every reason to be pleased.

“This will raise Mount Hua’s status once again.”

“What a cliché! It’s not just Mount Hua! The status of the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Tang Family will also rise again.”

Tang Moon-hyuk chuckled.

“Thinking about how those Nine Great Sects bastards, especially that Shaolin bald guy, will be furious already makes me feel like a decade-old blockage is clearing.”

“Right, right!”

The gathered people burst into loud laughter.

They had never had good feelings toward the Nine Great Sects, including the Shaolin sect. Due to regional characteristics, the Tang Family opposed the Qingcheng sect, backed by the Nine Great Sects. The resentment peaked after the Plum Blossom Island incident, so such reactions were natural.

“But... where are our heroes who accomplished such great deeds? I heard Miss Soso also came back?”

“Tsk tsk. That’s obvious. They must be receiving praise from the higher-ups right now.”

“I suppose so?”

“Is this an ordinary matter? It’s not just anywhere, but the Heavenly Friends Alliance. They crossed the enemy’s territory in the Southern lands, achieved merit, and returned. They deserve praise all day and night for seven days straight!”

“Heh. I’m jealous. I wish I could get praised by the family head once.”

“You won’t get that in your lifetime, so don’t get your hopes up and just be careful not to get cursed.”

“What?”

The atmosphere became lively. Though they hadn’t accomplished the feat themselves, as members of the same Heavenly Friends Alliance, it was enough to ignite their pride. Everyone gathered in groups to praise Chung Myung of the Mount Hua’s Sword Saint and those who supported him.

They did so without a hint of doubt that they were receiving praise fitting such a tremendous achievement.

And at that moment.

“Listen carefully.”

“...”

“I said listen carefully.”

The heroes of Mount Hua who boldly entered the den of the Four Sects Alliance in the Southern lands, defeated the Demonic Cult members massacring civilians, and escaped that brutal Southern Lands without casualties... were lined up kneeling with their arms raised.

Baek Cheon, silently bowing his head deeply, glanced sideways at Hyun Jong seated at the head seat. Seeing the blue veins bulging on his forehead like a snake, Baek Cheon quietly lowered his eyes again.

“I’ve... lived a long life...”

“...”

“What? They gave you a pirate ship and you just rode it back?”

“...”

“You tore apart the pirate ship to cook food and set it on fire?”

Hyun Jong’s eyes gleamed with killing intent.

Of course, this Hyun Jong was not the one who had been sleepless with worry over his disciples.

Upon hearing that a burnt pirate ship was found after a fierce battle, he rushed a thousand li upriver to Nanjing, then heard those he was worried about had arrived at the mouth of the river on the ship and were partying, so he ran another thousand li and just arrived at the mouth of the river.

“Heh heh... heh. Hehehe...”

Hyun Jong, laughing incredulously, suddenly rolled his eyes and lunged forward.

“Are you people even human beings!”

“Catch them!”

“Stop them!”

As Hyun Jong lunged, Tang Gunak and Hyun Sang quickly moved to restrain him. Hyun Jong, dangling in their grasp, repeatedly kicked the air.

“I thought the one remaining kid was rotting inside! What? You came on a pirate ship? And you were hungry so you tore it apart? You bastards worse than the Four Sects Alliance!”

“P-Please calm down, Alliance lord!”

“Oh, sect leader! Please hold back! They did come back after achieving good deeds, didn’t they?”

“Good? Good deeds? Damn it! So what if they achieved merit? They’re still immature as ever! People!”

Hyun Jong’s powerful kicks repeatedly brushed dangerously close to Baek Cheon’s face, the wind pressure striking his pale face.

But Baek Cheon dared not pull his head back and only averted his gaze.

‘I didn’t know anymore...’

Who would have known Hyun Jong wouldn’t wait at the mouth of the river but would roam around the Yangtze River? They thought it would be fine just to approach the mouth of the river and set up communication means in advance.

They never dreamed that Hyun Jong, burning with worry, would go upriver while they were warming themselves by the fire on their way back.

“Well, if you’re hungry... we should have eaten something...”

“Shut up, you bastard!”

“Be quiet!”

“Shut your mouth!”

“You don’t open your mouth! Never open your mouth! I’ll kill you for real!”

The Five Swords were startled and unleashed a barrage of words at Chung Myung.

“Did I say something wrong?”

“Just shut up!”

Chung Myung, verbally beaten, pouted.

Unlike Chung Myung, the other Five Swords had no words even if they had ten mouths.

Think about it. They insisted on playing in the water despite being told not to, went to the riverbank, and then heard the kid drowned. They rushed to search all day, only to find the kid was peacefully playing at home. How would a parent feel? Surely their heart would rot with worry.

‘If it were me, I’d at least beat their backs to shreds first.’

‘The sect leader is truly a sage. He spared us.’

Hyun Jong glared at Un Geom with murderous eyes.

“And you! You bastard! You!”

Un Geom bowed his head even lower.

“I sent you along to prevent this from happening! What? You were just dying there together? Did that go down your throat? Did it?”

Un Geom’s forehead almost touched the ground. Seeing his back curled like a snail inside its shell, the Five Swords couldn’t hold back tears.

‘Sasuk.’

‘I’m sorry, senior sasuk.’

‘Oh dear...’

Un Geom had a mouth too, but now he completely lost the ability to speak. If only he had stopped briefly by the river to set up a communication line while leisurely going upriver, things wouldn’t have escalated like this. Missing that simple step caused the master and other disciples to travel all the way to Nanjing.

Thinking about how worried they must have been rushing to Nanjing at full speed...

“I’m sorry...”

“Oh my fate! How did three generations...”

They sent the Un disciples, Baek disciples, and Chung disciples together to prevent accidents. Yet all three did the same thing side by side. At this point, shouldn’t they seriously consider if the sect is cursed?

Of course, Un Geom had some grievances. Still, he couldn’t utter a word because there were others here who were even more wronged.

“Even the young family lord does the same!”

“Huh?”

Namgung Do-Wei, suddenly hit by the unexpected remark, widened his eyes. He had been sitting in a corner, cautiously kneeling.

“If your group is doing crazy things, shouldn’t you stop them first? How can the young family lord of the Namgung Family do the same as these reckless bastards!”

Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes grew even wider.

“M-Me?”

“Exactly!”

“These people?”

“Well...”

“Me?”

“...”

“Me?”

Hyun Jong’s face lost its soul. Just from that reaction, one could understand what Namgung Do-Wei had seen and experienced.

And then, as if unable to comprehend the situation, Chung Myung spoke.

“I told you we would go and come back safely, so don’t worry and just wait. Maybe the sect leader is getting old and worrying unnecessarily.”

“...”

“Anyway, everyone came back safely, so isn’t that enough?”

At that moment, everyone present saw Hyun Jong’s face, blankly staring at Chung Myung, gradually change and twist like a demonic spirit.

It was a sight the disciples of Mount Hua had never seen before. The word ‘escape’ flashed strongly in their minds.

“Aaaah! You crazy bastards!”

Finally exploding, Hyun Jong threw off Tang Gunak and Hyun Sang who held his arms and lunged at the Five Swords.

Long after... Hyun Sang recalled that he saw an Asura there.

Chapter 1087: What Sin Did I Commit In My Previous Life? (2)

The moment Hyun Jong, who had been bowing his head deeply, raised it, the disciples of Mount Hua slammed their heads to the ground like lightning.

“If you make eye contact, you die.”

“That is not a joke, you really die.”

Cold sweat broke out on the backs of the Mount Hua disciples. They had seen Hyun Jong angry many times before, but this was a different kind of anger altogether.

“The high priest would have been better.”

“Please save me!”

From Hyun Jong’s mouth, who was emitting killing intent from his eyes, suddenly came a long sigh.

“I...”

“...”

“What sin did I commit in my previous life...”

Un Geom and Baek Cheon lowered their heads even further.

“Ughhh.”

Hyun Jong scratched his head vigorously. Though he wanted to overturn everything in anger, thinking about how much hardship these guys had endured made it hard to get angrier.

To be honest, were these guys in a situation to be scolded? They were heroes who had achieved feats worthy of a city-wide welcome in other sects. Yet these heroes...

“...”

Seeing the disciples bowing their heads to avoid eye contact and Chung Myung sending a look like “He’s really being difficult,”...

“Ugh...”

His neck stiffened naturally. He tried to find some herbal medicine, but even Tang Soso, who should prepare it, sat sullenly in the corner. Hyun Jong sighed again.

“It’s all my fault. Yes, all my fault...”

“...”

“Everyone...”

Hyun Jong bit his lip slightly.

“Still, it’s a relief. Everyone returned safely.”

“No, you should at least let us lower our arms before saying that...”

“Ah, shut up!”

“Soso! Where’s the needle? Needle it in that bastard’s mouth!”

“You just die already!”

The Mount Hua disciples rolled their eyes and glared at Chung Myung. If that bastard had talked less, the scolding would have been half as much.

Hyun Jong sighed deeply.

“Alright, everyone lower your hands.”

“Hehe.”

“You keep yours up.”

“...Why only me?”

“Just keep it up.”

Chung Myung’s lips protruded. He was full of dissatisfaction as he sluggishly raised his arm again.

The others quietly lowered their arms.

Watching this, Hyun Jong blamed himself inwardly.

‘How foolish I am.’

He wasn’t angry because he had traveled a thousand miles in vain. The real anger surged the moment he saw the state of those wandering here.

Their clothes were torn and ragged in places, even stained with dried blood. The scabs glimpsed through the torn clothes hinted at the fierce battles they had endured.

Seeing that, a thousand fires flared up in his heart.

He was proud. Of course, proud. He understood well why they went to Gangnam. He could not deny the value of what they protected and the importance of the agreement they upheld.

But still, he was angry.

No matter how noble the cause, what parent would like their child going to a dangerous battlefield? Seeing those guys smiling after surviving such harsh battles made his anger surge uncontrollably.

Hyun Jong quietly bowed his head.

“Everyone... thank you.”

Then everyone was flustered and opened their mouths wide.

“S-sect leader!”

“Ah, no, why are you like this! We were wrong.”

“Beg, you bastards! Beg quickly!”

Hyun Jong, raising his head, shook it at the flustered disciples who didn’t know what to do.

“I’m not thanking you for what you did.”

“...”

“You resolved the crisis in the Central Plains, elevated Mount Hua’s prestige, and showed the entire murim world that the Righteousness still exists.”

“Sect leader...”

Hyun Jong looked at his disciples with sunken eyes.

“But I am not thankful for any of that.”

“...”

“There is only one thing I am thankful to you for.”

He bowed his head once more.

“For returning safe and sound... I truly, truly thank you.”

The disciples of Mount Hua bowed their heads together.

It was clear how much Hyun Jong worried and agonized over them. Looking back, he had only worried about the safety of those fighting inside, never considering the feelings of those left behind. That was probably why he even forgot to send a simple message.

A fresh feeling of guilt arose. The Mount Hua disciples sighed repeatedly.

“Sorry, Sect leader.”

“Sorry.”

“Can we lower our hands now?”

“No.”

“Ugh, really.”

Chung Myung grumbled, but his voice didn’t reach anyone else’s ears.

“Phew.”

When Hyun Jong sighed deeply, Hyun Young beside him clicked his tongue.

“Well, when kids fight wildly, mistakes happen. Don’t be petty about it.”

“What, you brat?”

“Anyway, you get more childish as you age.”

Hyun Sang, watching cautiously nearby, gently stopped Hyun Young.

“Isn’t the sect leader simply worried?”

“Who here isn’t worried about the situation? If the kids came back safely, you should praise them first. That’s what Sect leader should do!”

“Ugh, ugh...”

Hyun Jong had nothing to say and turned his head sharply. Hyun Young clicked his tongue and looked at the kids.

“Next time, think about the person who waits anxiously. I thought the sect leader was going to die.”

“I will keep that in mind, Elder.”

“You definitely will.”

Hyun Young nodded.

“Alright. Now that sect leader seems to have calmed down, tell me what happened.”

Those seated glanced at Baek Cheon, as if telling him to speak on behalf.

Baek Cheon cleared his throat and began to speak.

“First of all...”

The events experienced in the Southern land slowly began to unfold.

“Hmm.”

After hearing the not-so-short story, a groan escaped Hyun Jong’s lips. Everyone looked at him, curious about what he would say first.

“Un Geom.”

“Yes, sect leader.”

“Where are the woman and child you rescued from Hangzhou now?”

Hearing that, smiles naturally appeared on the Mount Hua disciples’ faces. It was so like Hyun Jong.

“The woman and child are resting in a quiet room.”

Un Geom answered calmly and added.

“They have no particular physical abnormalities but are in a very weakened state. Even if we don’t know, for an ordinary woman, going against the water current wouldn’t be easy.”

“That’s right, that’s right.”

“So, I thought resting in a comfortable place was the priority. I asked the Tang family physicians, so there should be no problem.”

“Good. The child is safe too?”

“Yes, Sect leader. You don’t need to worry.”

“That’s a relief.”

Only then did Hyun Jong sigh lightly.

Compared to the rise of the Demonic Cult and the unification of the Four Sects Alliance, two huge events sure to throw the murim world into chaos, the safety of just two survivors might seem trivial.

But Hyun Jong mentioned the safety of those two first among all matters. That revealed what kind of person he was, positively or negatively.

At that moment, Tang Soso quietly spoke.
